# Second opinion (Phase 4.6), M6 T26: EmulationStation's main menu (GuiMenu), in pixelelated's EmulationStation fork

You are the one independent reviewer of a read-only renovation audit of two files, read under a **new-functionality and brittleness lens**. They are `es-app/src/guis/GuiMenu.cpp` (11,747 lines) and `GuiMenu.h` (136) at the EmulationStation commit `16dbac5211` (the distribution pins that commit). The fork rewrote them from ROCKNIX's base: 5,124 insertions over 225 commits. Every page the fork added is built in this one file:

- the cloud hub and its transfer options and system picker;
- the SSH setup wizard and the folder pages;
- the provider form and the phone sign-in with its QR code;
- the connections pages and the post-restore relink page;
- the network pages.

**What ships.** The product is an immutable handheld OS; a person updates it in place and keeps `/storage`.

- EmulationStation is its interface, at 640x480 on the smallest panel, run as root.
- The two stores are `system.cfg` (`SystemConf`, the launch scripts' store) and `es_settings.cfg` (`Settings`, EmulationStation's own).
- The cloud scripts this file runs (`cloud_remote`, `cloud_setup`, `cloud_oauth`, `cloud_scan`) and the OS scripts it meets (`setsettings.sh`, `001-functions`, `setrootpass`) are other slices' files. They are read here at the distribution commit `8ebaf8daad`.
- The rules the code answers to are named in `03` § 3.2.

**What you get.**

- The first reader is another lab's model. You get its `02-forward-audit.md`, `03-retrospective.md` and `04-analysis.md` whole.
  - `02`'s tables are generated by tools the first reader wrote; each tool's method is stated in `02` beside its table.
  - The generated tables are kept whole here: the pages, the rows, the transitions, the reads, the duplicates and the merge debt.
- Then context: the brief's questions, and the register rows the code cites.
- Then the host fixture's output and the guest window's records.
- Then `GuiMenu.h` whole, and **line-numbered excerpts of `GuiMenu.cpp`**. These cover every line the analyses cite with eight lines of context, plus the whole of the fork's added definitions and classes (`:4086-8497`, `:9744-10512`, `:296-392`, and the main menu's constructor).
  - The unchanged upstream regions the lens does not reach are trimmed (D-WORKFLOW-195): the BATOCERA pages, the theme page, the per-system configuration page outside its cited lines, and the reset and system settings pages outside their cited lines. The packet's manifest names every range sent.
- Then excerpts of the neighbours the leads turn on: `GuiSettings.cpp`, `GuiMsgBox.cpp`, `FileSystemUtil.cpp`, `Platform.cpp`, `TextureResource.cpp`, `ImageComponent.cpp`, `SwitchComponent.cpp`, `ComponentList.cpp`, `Settings.cpp`, `IList.h`, `StringUtil.cpp` and `CloudText.cpp`. Then the scripts at `8ebaf8daad`, and T21's later change to `GuiSettings.cpp` (`3ae9610b0`, not in the pin).
- **When a claim needs lines outside the packet, say `outside the packet` and do not guess.**

**Masking.** Every section was passed through a mask that replaces a value after `key=`, `key:`, `token=`, `user=`, `pass...=` or `psk=` with `***`. A `***` is the mask, not the code. The packet was scanned for credential shapes before it left; no hit was a value.

**Your job is to refute, narrow or confirm, against the code: not to agree.** Cite `file:line` for every claim, and say plainly where the first reader is wrong. Its line numbers are the pin's.

**What was run.** Nothing can be run for you.

- Ten harnesses compiled the pin's own functions on the host and pass.
- One fixture ran `Platform.cpp`'s `GetShOutput` verbatim.
- Five generators produced `02`'s tables. Each asserts its counts and was seen to fail on a constructed drift.
- A guest window ran an image whose EmulationStation is a **later composition over the pin** (it carries both the pin's literals and the later ones). Four of the five scripts the proofs touch are byte-identical to `8ebaf8daad`. So a guest proof is the image's, and `02` § The guest cycle says which.

Answer these first, in this order:

1. **The rows and their stores (Q2; L-07 to L-15).**
   - Is any value-binding row's store or key wrong beyond L-09 and L-10, by the rows table and the code?
   - Is the generator's assignment of writes to rows wrong anywhere you can see (an if/else pair, a hook naming several components, a local standing for a component)?
   - Is L-07's claim right: that T21's change to `buildInputTextConfigRow` guards every `addInputTextConfigRow` caller in this file?
   - Is L-08 right about the ROCKNIX SECURITY page?
2. **The interface thread (Q3; L-04).**
   - Is any process start on the interface thread missing from `02` § The pages, or wrongly bounded or threaded?
   - Which of them can a player meet with no network, a wedged `rclone`, or a NetworkManager that does not answer, and for how long?
3. **The reads (Q4; L-01, L-02, L-03, L-30).**
   - Is any cached read of a file another process writes missing?
   - Is L-02's route right, and is it reachable on any image the code allows?
4. **The pages' lifetimes (Q1; L-16, L-17).**
   - Is any transition, posted rebuild or detached thread unsound, beyond what `02` § The transitions records?
   - Is L-17's "safe by a self-capture cycle" right?
5. **The words and the dialogs (Q5; L-18 to L-25).**
   - Is any other string naming a row, page or menu path that the pin does not build?
   - Is any other player-facing text unreachable by the French catalog?
   - Is any back-button landing on a destructive choice?
6. **The commands and their readers (Q6; L-05, L-06, L-27).**
   - Is any command built from a string that is not quoted?
   - Is any reader of a command's output wrong in a way the first reader missed?
7. **The structure and the merge debt (Q7; L-26, L-28, L-29).** Is anything recorded here wrong?

Then list, as **new items**, any defect the first reader missed, each with `file:line`, the family (a page's lifetime or thread; a read that can be stale; a row that writes or reads the wrong place; a word or a dialog that breaks the house rules), and what would prove it.

**The shape of the answer.** One item per lead or finding, numbered `GM-1`, `GM-2`, ... Each item:
- names the lead (or `new`);
- gives a verdict: `confirmed`, `narrowed`, `refuted` or `new`;
- gives the evidence at `file:line`;
- says what the first reader should change, if anything.

Close with the items you would rank highest, and why.

## The pages

Generated by `tools/t26-pages.py` (its method in its docstring). A page is a `new GuiSettings(`, a class deriving from `GuiSettings`, or the main menu; its range is the innermost block that holds its construction. **Runs** are the process primitives and this file's helpers around them (page builders are openers, not runs), with the thread and the bound; **dialogs** give the buttons in order and where back lands. Generated by `tools/t26-rows.py`, with the ROCKNIX column from `tools/t26-structure.py --lines`. A value-binding row is a builder that binds a key itself (`addSwitch`, `addOptionList`, `addInputTextConfigRow`, `addFileBrowser`) or a component row whose component a store write in a save hook names; the method, and how a write is given to a row, are in the tool's docstring. The OS column marks a literal `system.cfg` key a script writes (`set_setting`), names (`named`) or asks for by its `global.` suffix (`suffix`), else `-`. The keys both this file and an OS script write are the rows marked `set_setting`.

## The transitions

Generated by `tools/t26-structure.py --transitions`: every `cloudSetupPresent` call (sixteen) and every lambda that ends one page and opens another in the same lambda. **Held by** is the receiver of the call the lambda is passed to; a capture used after the close is a use-after-free only when the holder is the closed page or one of its rows (blindspot 4). **Harness** names the tests under the ES tree's `tests/` that name the enclosing function.

Read at their lines: the sixteen `cloudSetupPresent` calls are each their scope's last statement but `:7862`, where the blocking sign-in window is opened after the page is on the stack and the function uses only its own copies (`:7864-7868`). Of the ten other replacements, the delete in `:1616`, `:10149` and `:10856` runs inside the closed page's own row's callback (L-17); `:3294` and `:9537` delete the `GuiMenu`, not the page that holds the lambda; `:8234` closes the chooser from the picker's row (its comment says so); `:5156` closes the page from `run`, which copies `window` first (`:4967`). Every dialog's callback runs on its own copy (`GuiMsgBox.cpp:286-294`).

Who writes each file another process writes: `rclone.conf` (`cloud_remote`, `cloud_setup`, the wizard), the `last-*` stamps (the cloud scripts and `ThreadedCloudSync`), the restore marker (`backuptool`), `JourneyTiers::PATH` (this file, `:5066`), the scan and selection files (`cloud_scan`, `cloud_content_restore --set-systems`) -- read through `cloudSelectionRead`'s cached `isRegularFile` (L-03) -- and `/tmp/cloud-oauth-qr.png` (`qrencode`, run from this file, L-01). The rest are binaries on the read-only image (`/usr/bin/cloud_*`, `gpudriver`, `dtb_overlay`), a sysfs node, the theme settings ES writes itself, and the wireguard config.

## Positives

- **Every wizard transition hands the new page on** (§ The transitions): fifteen of sixteen `cloudSetupPresent` calls are their scope's last statement and the sixteenth is deliberate; five are covered by `cloud-oauth-lifetime.py` and `cloud-gated-row.py`, and `cloud-folder-reopen.py` covers the folder page's rebuild.
- **A dialog's callback runs on its own copy** (`GuiMsgBox.cpp:286-294`): closing a page from one cannot free the running lambda.
- **The posted rebuilds are weakly held or owned by the page**: the picker's (`:4529-4584`), the transfer options' cycle broken at `onFinalize` (`:5202-5206`), `CloudConnectionsPage`'s guarded by `mAlive` (`:5580-5594`).
- **The long cloud calls run behind a spinner**: the match preview (`:5288-5296`), CHECK CONNECTION (`:5688-5709`), the checks (`:6636-6654`), the folder validator (`:6976-6986`), CONNECT (`:7404-7433`), the connection change (`:7994-8005`), the sign-in's wait (`:7624-7635`), the saved networks (`:10495-10511`).
- **The cloud folder step is the retired migration's replacement**: CHECK FOLDERS reads only, CREATE FOLDERS asks first, `stillSelected` refuses a press on paths that changed (`:6883-7057`); no join, follow or move is built (D-CLOUD-175/178/179).
- **The gated row re-checks at the press** (`:6313-6321`, audit #307 PL-062).
- **Credentials**: the form masks password and sensitive fields alike (`:7285`); CONNECT's log is masked (`:7414`); `setrootpass` is quoted at all three sites and guarded at two (L-08 is the third).
- **The definitions match the header**: 62 declared, 62 defined.

## The #585 lines owed

Per D-WORKFLOW-164, each confirmed defect or reusable fix is weighed for upstream:

- L-09 (`global.videofilters`) and L-10 (`ScrollLoadMedias`) are upstream's rows in upstream's code: a one-word fix each, contained, with a public reproducer (the key a script reads against the key the row writes). Candidates for `ROCKNIX/emulationstation-next`; the behaviour change is the maintainer's to open (D-UI-079), the defect's removal ours (`bugs-are-agent-first.md` rule 6).
- L-05 (`GetShOutput`) is `es-core`'s, upstream's, and reaches every caller of it: a candidate for upstream with the fixture as its reproducer; the fork's own callers are this file's.
- L-02 (the texture cache by path) is a property of `TextureResource`, upstream's; the fork's QR page is the caller that meets it. The fix belongs in this file (a path per session), not upstream.
- The rest are the fork's own and stay here.

## What T31 to T37 inherit

Written, not tested here:

- **The page builders' pattern:** one function per page, built in place; a wizard page is shown by `cloudSetupPresent` as its scope's last statement, its callbacks handing the new page on (§ The transitions); a button bar is rebuilt only at build or posted, never from a button's own callback (`es-code-traps.md`).
- **The rows' store rule:** a row's store is its builder's `storeInSettings` or its save hook's writer; a key is written where its reader reads it (L-09, L-10 are what happens otherwise); a page of `addInputTextConfigRow` rows only needs its own save (L-12).
- **The readers' rule:** a file another process writes is read uncached (`exists(p, false)`), `isRegularFile` included (L-03); an image another page may still hold is asked for by a path that names its session (L-02).
- **The quoting function:** one algorithm, `Utils::String::shellQuote` (L-27).
- **The commands' reader:** `GetShOutputLines`, not `GetShOutput` (L-05).
- **The upstream hunks table:** § The merge debt, with `cada856d8` first.



## The first reader's 03-retrospective.md (whole)

# Retrospective audit — M6.P2 T26, EmulationStation's main menu, GuiMenu (#673)

Written by the first reader (Opus 5.5) after stages A and B and the guest window, before any seat saw the packet. Line numbers are the pin's (`16dbac5211`) unless they name a script, which is `8ebaf8daad`'s. The leads are `02`'s (L-01 to L-30).

## 3.1 Architectural coherence

- **One file builds every fork page in place.** 97 of the file's 156 definitions are the fork's own, 3,599 lines, from the content picker (`:4086`) to MANAGE SAVED NETWORKS (`:10512`): the cloud pages, the wizard, the sign-in, the connections, the relink page and the network pages. Each page is one function, and the two largest fork-own ones are over 300 lines (`cloudOpenTransferOptions` 375, `cloudContentSystemPicker` 317). The definitions table separates the cloud and network buckets cleanly, which is D-WORKFLOW-080's option (a) (`02` § The merge debt).
- **The wizard's lifetime discipline holds.** Every page change goes through `cloudSetupPresent` as its scope's last statement, hands the new page on, and closes the old one after the push. Dialog callbacks run on their own copies (`GuiMsgBox.cpp:286-294`). The posted rebuilds are weakly held or owned by their page. The 2026-09-05 shape (a callback capturing a deleted predecessor) is gone (`02` § The transitions).
- **The thread rule holds for the long calls and not for the short ones.** Every network-bound cloud call runs behind `GuiLoading`. The short local ones run on the interface thread with no bound: 116 starts (`cloud_remote list`/`providers`/`fields`, `--connected`, `cloud_oauth status`, `systemctl`). Each is usually quick and none is bounded, so a wedged `rclone` or NetworkManager freezes the menu with nothing on screen (L-04).
- **Reads of other processes' files are mostly uncached, and two are not.** The twelve `exists(p, false)` reads cover the stamps, `rclone.conf` and the markers. The QR (`:7792`) and the scan and selection files (`:4136`, through `isRegularFile`) read the cache, which remembers a miss (L-01, L-03). The QR's miss was shown on the guest with a control.
- **Two readers of one shell output.** `GetShOutput` drops a byte per read; the fork knew (`:7127-7131`) and moved the line readers to `GetShOutputLines`, but the create/update and select/remove paths still read through it, matching sentences by substring (L-05, fixture).

## 3.2 Project conformance

- **`least-surprise.md` and `player-language.md`:** a failure dialog sends the player to a row that does not exist (L-18, guest), the match's refusal and two provider labels reach a French player in English (L-19, L-20, guest), and the Wi-Fi verdict is said three different ways (L-26).
- **`es-player-text.md` § "Every fork string ships in English and French":** the script-supplied refusal (L-19) and the runtime msgid (L-20). The fork's 706 extracted strings all have French.
- **`es-ui-style-guide.md` § Saving:** "apply a credential only when it is non-empty *and* changed". DEVICE PASSWORD and SSH PASSWORD do (`:8417`, `:6533`); the ROCKNIX SECURITY page's ROOT PASSWORD runs `setrootpass` on every close (L-08). The six relink rows are fixed by T21's S1 (L-07).
- **`es-ui-style-guide.md` § Text:** the fork's MANUAL UPDATES dialog is sentence-case prose with a URL (L-21).
- **`upgrade-and-install.md`, "does any string still name the old path?":** `c8dc72cf9` renamed the row and updated one of the two sentences naming it (L-18): the sweep `decision-register.md` asks of a rename covered fewer sites than it changed (blindspot 57's shape).
- **D-UI-039:** the menu map's relink heading is stale against D-UI-046 (L-23); `es-menu-map-check` passes because it checks titles, not headings.
- **D-CLOUD-175/178/179:** the pin builds no join, follow or move; the folder step is CHECK and CREATE with explicit confirmation (`02` § Positives).

## 3.3 Spec fidelity

The brief's figures held at their hashes (`01` § Scope, `02` § The runs). Four of its statements were corrected by command and received as errata at 08:06 UTC: MANUAL UPDATES is the fork's (`975235429`); the RGB LED page and the first SECURITY page are `BATOCERA`-only, never built on ROCKNIX (closing T21's `:2989` and `:3209` notes); the six unguarded credential rows are already fixed by T21's S1; and 11 of the 33 "unread keys" hold once `get_setting`'s `global.` fallback is read. The digest's 15/44 definitions split is its body-text rule; the lane's exact-text rule reads 12/47 (`01` § Scope).

## 3.4 Platform conformance

- **The image is M7's composition over the pin** (G01: both literals). Four of the five scripts the proofs touch are `8ebaf8daad`'s bytes; `cloud_oauth` differs and its `ON_DEVICE` check is the tree's. The composition renames two of the hub's rows (MANAGE CLOUD CONNECTIONS, ADD FROM THIS DEVICE).
- **640x480 menus are full-screen**, so `cloudSetupPresent`'s sizing never runs on the panel the walks use (L-16 not reached).
- **The image's seed `system.cfg` carries `system.battery.warning_threshold=25`**, so the SOUND page's write-if-empty acts only on a device whose file lacks it (L-11).
- **`qrencode` and the sign-in window ship;** `ON_DEVICE=yes`, so L-02's route is not reached here.

## 3.5 Cross-system interaction audit

### Interaction: the global VIDEO FILTER row x `setsettings.sh` x `get_setting`

The row writes `global.videofilters` (`:5947`); the launch script asks `game_setting videofilter` (`setsettings.sh:793`), whose last fallback is `global.videofilter` (`001-functions:164-172`). The names differ by one letter, so the global choice never reaches a game (L-09, guest).

### Interaction: the fast-scrolling switch x `Settings` x `SystemConf`

`addSwitch(..., false)` stores in `system.cfg`; the list reads `Settings::ScrollLoadMedias()` from `es_settings.cfg` (`IList.h:110`, `:118`, `:418`). The switch shows and saves a value nothing reads (L-10, guest).

### Interaction: the QR page x the file cache x the texture cache

`qrencode` writes `/tmp/cloud-oauth-qr.png`; the page asks `exists` through the cache (L-01, guest with control) and asks `TextureResource` by path, which returns any live texture of that name (L-02, not reached on this image).

### Interaction: the relink page x the renamed row

The relink page's failure text names CONNECT OR REPAIR CLOUD STORAGE (`:8460`); the hub's row has been CLOUD CONNECTIONS since `c8dc72cf9` (MANAGE CLOUD CONNECTIONS on the image). The French catalog keeps the old msgids (L-18, guest in both languages).

### Interaction: the ROCKNIX SECURITY page x `setrootpass` x T21's S1

Every close runs `setrootpass` (`:3267-3273`). The script refuses an empty password, so the device's is safe; but after S1 a typed-empty entry is saved to `system.cfg` before the refusal, and the wizard's CURRENT PASSWORD then reads blank while the device keeps its password (L-08).

### Interaction: `cloud_remote` x `GetShOutput` x `cloudConnectionError`

The create and change paths merge stderr into stdout and read the result through a reader that drops one byte per 255 of a long line; a refusal sentence across that byte no longer matches, and the generic sentence shows (L-05, fixture).

## 3.6 Time to play

From `docs/vm-qa-log.md` (the September rows: interface to a game's first frame 0.6 to 1.11 s; `Not measured` on 2026-10-14 and -16). This slice changed nothing, and it names three costs it found:
- **The main menu's build at START:** `GuiMenu::GuiMenu` (`:160-277`) reads the stores, asks `ApiSystem::isScriptingSupported` (T21's) a dozen times and builds rows; no call of this file's starts a process there (`launchKodi` runs on a press, `:182`). The hasher at `:1214` is T20's and starts from FIND ALL GAMES on FRONTEND DEVELOPER OPTIONS, also a press.
- **A page's close before a launch:** every settings page's close writes `es_settings.cfg` (G05 showed a rewrite with no change) and, through `saveSystemConf`, `system.cfg` under the lock. The ROCKNIX SECURITY page's close also re-runs `setrootpass` and its service restarts (L-08), seconds on the interface thread.
- **Opening a cloud page:** the hub starts `cloud_remote list` and `cloud_setup --info` (10 s bound) on the interface thread; CLOUD CONNECTIONS starts `list` twice (L-04). None sits between a launch press and a frame.



## The first reader's 04-analysis.md (whole)

# Analysis — M6.P2 T26, EmulationStation's main menu, GuiMenu (#673)

The first reader's analysis (Opus 5.5), written after stages A and B and the guest window, before the seat saw the packet. § Second opinion follows the seat. The leads are `02`'s (L-01 to L-30); every line is the pin's (`16dbac5211`) unless it names a script (`8ebaf8daad`'s).

## Executive summary

The fork's own pages are built carefully where they were built to be. The wizard's page changes all hand the new page on (fifteen of sixteen `cloudSetupPresent` calls are their scope's last statement, the sixteenth deliberately so). Every dialog callback runs on its own copy. The long cloud calls run behind a spinner. The retired folder migration's replacement asks before it creates anything.

What does not hold is at the joins:
- **A row and its reader:** two upstream rows write a key nothing reads. The global VIDEO FILTER writes `global.videofilters`, where the launch script reads `videofilter` (L-09). The fast-scrolling switch saves to `system.cfg`, where its reader reads `es_settings.cfg` (L-10). Both proved on the guest.
- **A rename and its sentences:** after a failed check, the relink page sends the player to CONNECT OR REPAIR CLOUD STORAGE, a row renamed on 2026-10-11 (L-18, guest in English and French).
- **The interface and the cache:** the sign-in's QR read is cached, so one failed attempt leaves the session text-only (L-01, guest with a control).
- **The interface thread:** 116 process starts with no bound, the cloud pages' among them (L-04).
- **The French surface:** the match's refusal and two provider labels reach a French player in English (L-19, L-20).
- **A credential row:** the ROCKNIX SECURITY page runs `setrootpass` on every close (L-08).

Two leads of the brief are closed without a change here: T21's S1 already guards the six relink rows (L-07), and `led.colour` is in code no ROCKNIX image builds (L-14).

## Risk assessment

| Risk | Leads | Likelihood | Impact | Severity |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A player follows a dead path after a failed cloud check | L-18 | certain on that path (guest) | a player told to use a row that is not there, at the moment of a failure | Medium |
| A setting the player changes does nothing | L-09, L-10 | certain (guest) | the VIDEO FILTER default and the fast-scrolling choice are silently ignored | Medium |
| The interface freezes on a wedged helper | L-04 | low (each call is local) | a frozen menu with nothing on screen, for as long as the helper hangs | Medium |
| A sign-in without a code after one failed attempt | L-01 | low (needs a first failure) | a text-only page for the rest of the session; the address still shows | Low |
| The device password re-applied on every SECURITY close | L-08 | certain (tree) | seconds of frozen interface and service restarts; after S1, `root.password` reads empty while the device keeps it | Medium |
| A French player reads English | L-19, L-20 | certain where reached (guest for L-20) | a refusal they must read to act on, in a language not theirs | Low |
| A long error line loses a byte and the wrong sentence shows | L-05 | low | the generic sentence instead of the specific one | Low |
| The next upstream merge conflicts in `openNetworkSettings` | L-28 | certain at the next merge | a manual resolution against a competing saved-networks design | Low (planning) |

## Coverage boundary

- **Read whole:** the 97 added definitions and the fork's three classes; the header. **At their lines:** the 12 changed definitions (fork hunks and every lead) and the 47 unchanged (every lead). **Every line:** the five generators.
- **On the guest:** the image is M7's composition over the pin, so every guest verdict is the image's; the steps listed in `02` § The guest cycle, at 640x480, in English and French. Not run: L-22's landing, L-16 at 1280x800.
- **Not this slice's:** `GuiSettings.cpp` (L-12's systemic fix), `Platform.cpp`'s `GetShOutput` (L-05's reader), `TextureResource` (L-02's cache), the cloud scripts (T17's), `setrootpass` (T1's): read and cited, not graded.
- **Not compiled here:** the host has no toolchain (`es-syntax-check` exit 2; `g++` stops at SDL); the ten harnesses compile extracted functions and passed.

## The questions

### Q1 The pages' lifetimes

**Sound, with one shape recorded.** The sixteen `cloudSetupPresent` calls hand the new page on; fifteen are their scope's last statement and `:7862` opens the blocking window after the push on purpose (`02` § The transitions; the ten harnesses rc 0). The posted rebuilds are weakly held (`:4529-4584`) or owned by the page (`:5202-5206`); `CloudConnectionsPage`'s posted refresh is guarded (`:5580-5594`); the three detached threads post through the gated `AppWindow::post` with weak rows (`:9769-9847`; `queryInterfaceAddresses` unbounded). The recorded shape: three callbacks delete the page whose row holds them, from inside that row's callback (`:10149-10153` the fork's; `:1616`, `:10855-10856` upstream's) -- safe by a self-capture cycle that leaks the switch, and by the input path returning at once at the two levels read (L-17). `cloud-oauth-lifetime.py` and `cloud-gated-row.py` cover five wizard transitions and `cloud-folder-reopen.py` the folder page's rebuild; the relink page's and the connections pages' transitions have no harness. L-16 (the 0.75 page unpositioned) is not reached at 640x480.

### Q2 The rows and their stores

275 value-binding rows (`t26-rows.py`), 267 store writes. **Two rows write where nothing reads** (L-09, L-10: guest). Eleven built rows write keys nothing reads once `get_setting`'s global fallback is counted; on the image two are hidden by runtime gates and PAUSE ON TRANSLATED SCREEN is reachable (L-13). The six `addInputTextConfigRow`-only pages persist through their `onFinalize` saves (L-12, the systemic fix `GuiSettings.cpp`'s). The six unguarded credential rows are fixed by T21's S1 (L-07). ROOT PASSWORD is unmasked and applied on every close (L-08). `led.colour` and the first SECURITY page are never built (L-14). `exitreboot` has no consumer on its page (L-15). Writes at open: two pages' (L-11), the image's seed making one moot. **The folder step** builds no join, follow or move: CHECK and CREATE, the latter confirmed (D-CLOUD-175/178/179).

### Q3 The interface thread's bounds

159 runs, 131 on the interface thread, 116 of those with no bound (`t26-pages.py`; L-04). The player sees nothing while each runs. The bounded ones are `cloudSetupInfo()` (10 s), `cloudScanStamped` (5 s), `cloudFolderContext()` (5 s) and the path editor (30 s, on a worker). None sits between a launch press and a frame (`03` § 3.6).

### Q4 The reads that can be stale

37 reads (`02` § The reads): 12 uncached. The cached ones that read a file another process writes are the QR (L-01, guest with a control) and the scan and selection files through `isRegularFile` (L-03, narrow). L-02 (a texture by path) is a second stale read on the same page, not reached on this image.

### Q5 The words and the dialogs

108 dialogs: 41 YES/NO landing on NO, 56 single OK. Off the house rules: L-18 (a dead row named, guest), L-19 and L-20 (English in French), L-21 (MANUAL UPDATES' case and URL, the fork's), L-22 (back lands on CANCEL SIGN-IN: a style call), L-23 (the map's stale heading), L-24 (upstream's strings, recorded), L-25 (HOSTNAME's blank). French: 978 of 980 one-line literals (the two are upstream's fuel gauge); 706 of 706 fork strings; 0 non-ASCII comments. The menu map: `0 missing`.

### Q6 The commands and their readers

`GetShOutput` drops a byte per read (fixture); the create and change paths read through it and match sentences by substring (L-05), with two parsers of one success line (L-06). One quoting algorithm in two functions (L-27). T18's PL-21 composers (`:5006`, `:6140`) and PL-17's discarded `backuptool backup` output (`:5125`) are cited. `credential-quoting.py`: 9 of 9 sites quoted.

### Q7 The structure and the merge debt

The ten longest functions are as the brief lists them; `openSystemSettings` is 1,604 lines. 74 labels are built in two or more functions; the FINISH RESTORE PROCESS row is built identically in two menus. 86 conditional regions, 869 lines no ROCKNIX image compiles, 13 regions whose first arm's braces do not balance (L-29). The Wi-Fi verdict is composed three ways (L-26). The merge debt: `cada856d8` meets the fork in all four of its hunks (L-28). D-WORKFLOW-080's option (a) is the shape the definitions table argues for.

## Renovation face

### Q1 Modernize

- Every short cloud call off the interface thread, or bounded, with a spinner where it can take longer than a frame (L-04).
- One output reader (`GetShOutputLines`) and one success parse for `cloud_remote` (L-05, L-06).
- The cloud and network pages out of `GuiMenu.cpp` into their own files (D-WORKFLOW-080's option (a)).

### Q2 Standards

- **`es-player-text.md` § French:** L-19, L-20. **`upgrade-and-install.md` (old paths in strings):** L-18. **`es-ui-style-guide.md` § Saving:** L-08. **§ Text:** L-21. **D-UI-039:** L-23. **`engineering-practices.md` § Guards must fail closed:** L-05's generic fallback hides the real refusal.

### Q3 Bugs

- **Medium:** L-08, L-09, L-10, L-18.
- **Low:** L-01, L-03, L-04 (Medium where a helper hangs), L-05, L-19, L-20, L-21, L-26.
- **Latent or not reached here:** L-02, L-16, L-17.

### Q4 Package currency

Nothing in these two files vendors a package. `qrencode` and the sign-in window ship on the image.

### Q5 Efficiency

The ROCKNIX SECURITY page's close re-applies the password and restarts services every time (L-08). A settings page's close rewrites `es_settings.cfg` even with no change (G05). CLOUD CONNECTIONS starts `cloud_remote list` twice at build.

### Q6 Dead ends

869 lines no ROCKNIX image compiles (L-29): the BATOCERA pages (the RGB LED page, the first SECURITY page, the torrent updates), the `!ROCKNIX` first arms and the WIN32 lambda copies. Recorded, not removed (D-UI-079). `:7422`'s `\r\n` search can never match (L-05).

### Q7 Clarity and structure

- `GuiMenu.cpp` is 11,747 lines of the main menu and every fork page; `openSystemSettings` alone is 1,604.
- The wizard's own comment calls `--check` "the only slow call" (`:6627-6630`), and `cloudSelectionRead`'s neighbour calls an `isRegularFile` read uncached (`:4154-4156`): two comments that state what the code does not do.

## Instruction File Recommendations

### Coverage gaps (would have prevented)

- **A rename's sweep greps the old name in `_()` strings and the French catalog** (L-18): `tools/archaeology --reversal` covers the tree, but `c8dc72cf9` updated one of two sentences. A `tools/vocabulary-check`-style check of row names cited in dialog text against the labels the pin builds would catch it mechanically.
- **A row's store matches its reader's** (L-10): `es-native-ui.md` already says `addSwitch`'s bool is `storeInSettings`; a generator like `t26-rows.py`'s OS column, run in CI, would catch a key no reader names.

### Codification gaps (proposals for the coordinator)

- `es-code-traps.md`: "`exists()` on a file a helper writes for this page" -- the QR's case (L-01) is the third of its kind after the stamps and the restore marker.
- `generic-x64-vm-testing.md`: `vm-serial script` takes no arguments; a `sh` call that reads stdin blocks the guest's shell; `system.language` takes `fr_FR`.

## Context 2: the register rows

```text
| D-UI-023 | 2026-09-06 | **A menu row is a label and at most one line under it; a description that would make a third line moves into the row's confirmation dialog where it is additive.** Maintainer, on 3.5- and 4-inch panels: *"adding a fuller description isn't necessarily always better ... we don't want to have lots of tiny text"*, and on the cloud settings rows that stacked what a row moves on top of how it last went: *"if the description can be moved into the confirmation dialog and it serves an additive function, that's the best-case scenario in principle (because it allows us to keep it to two lines max)."* Corollaries: a row that opens a page with more than one action is a submenu whose label carries the verb and whose page carries the choices; a row that genuinely needs explaining wants a page, not a longer line. Applied the same hour: the cloud hub row became MANAGE CLOUD STORAGE over its submenu's three section names; the sync row lost the two-way sentence that was already its dialog; the two saves rows keep only how they last went; the transfer page's tier rows keep only what they carry, because that page launches with no confirmation. Refines D-UI-017/018's three-line class rows, which had fixed a run-on sentence by making a stack. | maintainer, 2026-09-06; ES `257116b3bb`, `es-native-ui.md` |

| D-UI-039 | 2026-09-11 | **Settings do not stack in one window: a group that grows past a few rows nests behind a row of its own, and `docs/es-menu-map.md` is updated in the same change as every row added, moved or renamed.** Maintainer: "I don't want to start stacking too many settings in one window, so we may need to think about nesting some of what we're doing. This is one reason we should make sure we're updating our mapped mermaid or D2 file of everything we've done in EmulationStation." The map had last been touched 2026-09-09 and lagged five row changes; brought current the same day. | #134, #23 |

| D-UI-046 | 2026-09-12 | **The relink page and both rows that open it are called FINISH RESTORE PROCESS** (the cloud hub's row read FINALIZE RESTORE, NETWORK SETTINGS' row and the page read FINISH RESTORE SETUP; audit #129 PL-05). Maintainer: "why don't we make it both, say, 'finish restore process.'" | #129 |

| D-QA-021 | 2026-09-12 | **Every read of a device's or a QA guest's config goes through a secrets filter wider than the word *password*: `grep -v -i -E 'key\|pass\|token\|user\|psk'`.** A read needs no permission (D-QA-015) but lands in a transcript, and `system.cfg`, `rclone.conf`, `get_setting` output and anything piped back over SSH can carry a sign-in; rclone writes `pass =`, `user =`, `token =`, `key =`, and the network writes `psk`. The QA guests are in scope: their credentials are throwaway, their files are the same shape, and the transcript is the same transcript. A filter written as `password` let a `pass =` line through on a guest on 2026-09-12. Practice text moved into `engineering-practices.md` by the #147 sweep; long form in `docs/device-testing-policy.md` § Reading a device's output. (source: work log 2026-09-12 05:57 UTC, `6075c64f2e`; no maintainer quote on record) | #147, `6075c64f2e` |

| D-UI-079 | 2026-09-22 | **D-UI-078's foreground-page rule applies to the lanes the fork works in -- cloud sync and cloud behaviours, game saves, Wi-Fi, the offline achievements work -- and not to upstream surfaces outside them: the game index (`ThreadedHasher`), the content installer, the OS update and the Bluetooth scan keep their background cards for now.** Maintainer, 2026-09-22, closing #241's last box: *"We don't need to alter behaviors outside of the core work we've been tackling when it comes to the game index content installer, OS update, and Bluetooth scan. Let's not alter that work quite yet. I'm more comfortable modifying work within the different lanes we've been working in around cloud sync, cloud behaviors, game saves, Wi-Fi, etc."* The scraper was named in D-UI-078 itself and is done. | #241, D-UI-078 |

| D-CLOUD-175 | 2026-10-07 | **0.0.1 uses ordinary cloud linking, folder creation and explicit folder selection; retire the cloud-folder migration engine, automatic legacy joining/following, startup migration prompts and optional tidier. Fresh configurations default to `/pixelelated`; existing credentials and selected paths remain until explicitly changed. Users populate or rearrange their cloud folders themselves.** The maintainer says only their own installation has used the fork, raises multi-gigabyte ROMs and partially installed libraries, and says "I'm fine doing a rename on my end, and then we can abandon the migration entirely". This supersedes the move/follow/setup-step portions of D-CLOUD-158 through D-CLOUD-174 and D-UI-122 through D-UI-124; normal sync, selective transfer, defaults, stored-format guards and OS upgrade preservation remain. Historical correction: optional `/GAMES` to `/ROCKNIX` tidying was already included in the unmerged September 29 upstream PRs; automation came later. The owner's partially migrated Dropbox needs separately authorized host reconciliation, never an inferred rename/delete. | #508; supersedes #505's integration plan; ROCKNIX/distribution#3404 and ROCKNIX/emulationstation-next#40; source, UI, docs and focused VM sweep tracked by #508 |

| D-CLOUD-178 | 2026-10-07 | **Use a category validator for cloud setup, reusing existing backup/restore readers and interface building blocks. Validation itself is read-only; fixes are separate, explicit actions for game saves, save states and screenshots, with their exact plan, collision handling and verification defined before implementation. ROMs and BIOS get expected paths and manual setup instructions, never automatic library relocation.** Distinguish missing, empty, recognized misplaced and unreadable content within selected roots; folder readiness does not prove complete transfer integrity or BIOS compatibility. Preserve configured choices and do not infer cloud-wide absence. Once relevant wiki guides are published, `See instructions` opens a QR modal for the ROM or BIOS guide; no clickable-link or handheld-browser expectation. Until then, local guidance remains useful and no dead QR destination ships. Refines D-CLOUD-175/176/177; #510's direction is chosen, while #508 still needs contract reconciliation, implementation and qualification. | #510; #508; #511; maintainer's validator, reuse and QR guidance on 2026-10-07; `docs/pixelelated/cloud-folder-flow-review.md` |

| D-CLOUD-179 | 2026-10-08 | **The post-rclone setup solution is settled: place category verification, separately confirmed directory creation and explicit user actions after remote connection; use manual guidance for data-intensive ROM/BIOS organization. Make the remaining targeted game-save/save-state/screenshot repairs concrete through source-derived cases and bounded plans, not another generic design-choice question.** #515 owns that investigation and supported implementation/qualification before #508 final integration and #507's frozen delta audit; unsupported cases retain instructions with an evidence-backed disposition. The owner will manually align their experimental cloud, so no product compatibility branch or agent-led Dropbox reconciliation is required for it. Their reported experimental reset is context, not verified cloud integrity or a reset of device pointers. Refines D-CLOUD-175/177/178. | #510; #515; #508; maintainer's 2026-10-08 documentation/implementation request and Dropbox reset report; sweep: M7, #507/#508/#510, canonical session, cloud flow/menu/changelog and cloud/upgrade rules |

| D-WORKFLOW-195 | 2026-10-24 | **An Extended seat whose answer the model's output ceiling cuts on the packet is run again on a trimmed packet for that seat -- the same model, effort and provider; the analyses and tables whole, the cited files as line-numbered excerpts with context, the ask bounded -- never a model or effort substitution and never the same packet again.** `04` § Second opinion says the seats saw different evidence; an item one seat saw only in excerpt carries `excerpt`, and a disagreement that could be the excerpt's doing is resolved from the artifact, not from either seat. A fork resolved during execution on T30 (#677): Argon at `high` spent about 57k of its 65.5k output tokens reasoning on a 438 KB packet three times (`finish=length`), where T3's 308 KB packet took 30k. The maintainer may reverse it by a new row. | #677, #798; `adversarial-council.md` § Council model floor; the T30 hold post of 10:04 UTC and the coordinator's answer the same hour |

| D-WORKFLOW-080 | **How does the interface work go upstream in pieces a person can review?** D-WORKFLOW-066 sent it as one PR because `GuiMenu.cpp` and `ApiSystem.cpp` carry every bucket's rows and a per-bucket split was called the artificial cut; a ROCKNIX developer closed that PR (200 files, 41,496 insertions: *"how do you expect us to be able to review this? please can you separate the changes and use less AI to submit PR's"*), which reverses the premise. Three ways, the maintainer's to choose: (a) move each bucket's code out of `GuiMenu.cpp` into files of its own first (a mechanical refactor that is itself one reviewable PR), then seven PRs of disjoint files by the review guide's buckets; (b) split the shared files by hunk, which `tools/pr-stack-check` cannot build by content and a rebase breaks; (c) fewer, still large PRs (mechanics first, then the cloud, then the rest), each carrying its share of the shared files whole. My recommendation is (a). | #333; raised 2026-09-29 |
```

```text
  278  
  279  // A maintenance script run headlessly, with the outcome on a dialog.
  280  //
  281  // The ten rows on this page -- the two settings-backup rows and the eight
  282  // resets -- used to hand their command to /usr/bin/run: EmulationStation was
  283  // stopped, the script scrolled past on a console for as long as it ran, and
  284  // the UI came back. Nothing carried the result. runSystemCommand double-forks
  285  // and always returns 0, and /usr/bin/run exited 0 on both halves of its
  286  // console branch whatever the command did, so a factory reset that reset
  287  // nothing and one that worked were the same press with the same ending: the
  288  // carousel (#119).
  289  //
  290  // executeScriptLegacy hands back the real status and every line the script
  291  // printed, so the work happens behind a spinner and ends in a message. The
  292  // scripts print the why in the player's words at the point of failure
  293  // (D-UI-028), so the failure sentence is the script's own last line where
  294  // there is one, and the row's sentence only where the script died without
  295  // saying anything.
  296  static std::string maintenancePlainLine(const std::string& line)
  297  {
  298  	// The scripts colour their failure sentence for whoever is reading a
  299  	// console, and the cloud pages' ">>> " protocol lines are not sentences.
  300  	// Neither belongs on a dialog.
  301  	std::string out;
  302  	for (size_t i = 0; i < line.size(); i++)
  303  	{
  304  		if (line[i] == 0x1B)
  305  		{
  306  			while (i < line.size() && line[i] != 'm')
  307  				i++;
  308  			continue;
  309  		}
  310  		out += line[i];
  311  	}
  312  	return Utils::String::trim(out);
  313  }
  314  
  315  // The configuration on disk is no longer the one this process holds: a
  316  // settings restore has replaced it and a factory reset has removed it. Re-read
  317  // both before anything can write the old values back over what just happened
  318  // -- ViewController::saveState() saves es_settings.cfg on the way out whenever
  319  // the player has moved to another system since this boot, and that write
  320  // carries every other setting with it (#114).
  321  //
  322  // Re-reading is not enough after a factory reset: there is nothing to read,
  323  // Settings::loadFile leaves the old map as it stood, and the exit's
  324  // ViewController::saveState would write it all back with LastSystem. So the
  325  // exit is told to write nothing (#308 8-es claude F-ES-10). SystemConf needs
  326  // no telling: saveSystemConf refuses when the live file cannot be opened.
  327  static void maintenanceRestart()
  328  {
  329  	Settings::getInstance()->loadFile();
  330  	SystemConf::getInstance()->loadSystemConf();
  331  	ViewController::configurationReplaced();
  332  	Utils::Platform::quitES(Utils::Platform::QuitMode::REBOOT);
  333  }
  334  
  335  // What a failed maintenance run's dialog says after COULDN'T FINISH. The
  336  // why is the script's `>>> why` sentence (es-player-text.md, Outcome
  337  // vocabulary); backuptool's fail() prints a fuller one -- the same words,
  338  // then what is in place and how to recover -- as its last line, and that
  339  // one is said when it carries the why. Any other last line is a tool's,
  340  // printed after the why, and the why is said over it (#308 8-es claude
  341  // F-ES-13: the last line used to win whatever it was). A script that
  342  // printed no why is said by its last line, as before.
  343  static std::string maintenanceWhy(const std::string& last, const std::string& why)
  344  {
  345  	if (why.empty())
  346  		return last;
  347  	if (Utils::String::startsWith(last, why))
  348  		return last;
  349  	return why;
  350  }
  351  
  352  static void runMaintenanceCommand(Window* window, const std::string& cmd, const std::string& busyTitle,
  353  	const std::string& doneText, const std::string& failText, std::function<void()> onCompleted = nullptr)
  354  {
  355  	window->pushGui(new GuiLoading<std::pair<int, std::string>>(window, busyTitle,
  356  		[cmd](auto gui)
  357  		{
  358  			std::string last;
  359  			std::string why;
  360  			int rc = ApiSystem::executeScriptLegacy(cmd, [&last, &why](const std::string line)
  361  			{
  362  				const std::string clean = maintenancePlainLine(line);
  363  				if (clean.empty())
  364  					return;
  365  				if (clean.rfind(">>> why ", 0) == 0)
  366  				{
  367  					why = Utils::String::trim(clean.substr(8));
  368  					return;
  369  				}
  370  				if (clean.rfind(">>> ", 0) == 0)
  371  					return;
  372  				last = clean;
  373  			}).second;
  374  
  375  			if (rc == 0)
  376  				return std::pair<int, std::string>(0, "");
  377  			return std::pair<int, std::string>(rc, maintenanceWhy(last, why));
  378  		},
  379  		[window, doneText, failText, onCompleted](std::pair<int, std::string> result)
  380  		{
  381  			if (result.first == 0)
  382  			{
  383  				window->pushGui(new GuiMsgBox(window, doneText, _("OK"),
  384  					[onCompleted] { if (onCompleted != nullptr) onCompleted(); }));
  385  				return;
  386  			}
  387  
  388  			const std::string why = result.second.empty() ? failText : Utils::String::toUpper(result.second);
  389  			window->pushGui(new GuiMsgBox(window, _("COULDN'T FINISH") + " - " + why, _("OK"), nullptr, GuiMsgBoxIcon::ICON_ERROR));
  390  		}));
  391  }

  393  void GuiMenu::openResetOptions()
  394  {
  395  	Window *window = mWindow;
  396  
  397  	auto s = new GuiSettings(mWindow, _("SYSTEM MANAGEMENT AND RESET").c_str());
  398  
  399  	s->addGroup(_("DATA MANAGEMENT"));
  400  	s->addEntry(_("BACK UP SETTINGS TO THIS DEVICE"), true, [window] {
  401  	window->pushGui(new GuiMsgBox(window, _("BACK UP YOUR SETTINGS TO /storage/roms/backup/?\n\nWI-FI AND ACCOUNT PASSWORDS ARE NOT INCLUDED. COPY THE FILE SOMEWHERE SAFE, OR BACK UP TO THE CLOUD FROM GAME SETTINGS > MANAGE CLOUD STORAGE."), _("YES"),
  402  		[window] {
  403  		// backuptool names the archive it wrote to the system log only, so
  404  		// the dialog says where the backups live rather than which file this
  405  		// one is -- a filename is not something to read off a 3.5-inch panel.
  406  		runMaintenanceCommand(window, "/usr/bin/backuptool backup", _("PACKING UP YOUR SETTINGS..."),
  407  			_("SETTINGS BACKED UP TO THIS DEVICE.\n\nCOPY IT SOMEWHERE SAFE, OR BACK UP TO THE CLOUD FROM GAME SETTINGS > MANAGE CLOUD STORAGE."),
  408  			_("THE BACKUP COULDN'T FINISH. YOUR LAST BACKUP IS UNCHANGED."));
  409  		}, _("NO"), nullptr));
  410  	});
  411  
  412  	s->addEntry(_("RESTORE SETTINGS FROM THIS DEVICE"), true, [window] {
  413  	window->pushGui(new GuiMsgBox(window, _("RESTORE SETTINGS FROM THE NEWEST BACKUP IN /storage/roms/backup/?\n\nYOUR EXISTING CONFIGURATION WILL BE OVERWRITTEN AND THE DEVICE WILL REBOOT. WI-FI AND ACCOUNT PASSWORDS MUST BE RE-ENTERED AFTERWARDS."), _("YES"),
  414  		[window] {
  415  		// --no-restart, and the restart happens after the message: left to
  416  		// itself backuptool sleeps five seconds and reboots, which takes the
  417  		// screen away at the moment it has the outcome on it (#114). Nothing
  418  		// restarts on a failure.
  419  		runMaintenanceCommand(window, "/usr/bin/backuptool restore --no-restart", _("RESTORING YOUR SETTINGS..."),
  420  			_("SETTINGS RESTORED. THIS DEVICE RESTARTS SO THEY TAKE EFFECT."),
  421  			_("THE RESTORE COULDN'T FINISH."), maintenanceRestart);
  422  		}, _("NO"), nullptr));
  423  	});
  424  
  425  	// The cloud copies of these live in GAME SETTINGS > CLOUD SETTINGS, which does the
  426  	// same job with the save data included. The two entries that used to be
  427  	// here backed up settings only and were a second, quieter path to the
  428  	// same operation.
  429  
  430  
  431  	s->addEntry(_("CLEAN GAMELISTS & REMOVE UNUSED MEDIA"), true, [window] {
  432  	window->pushGui(new GuiMsgBox(window, _("ARE YOU SURE?"), _("YES"), [&]
  433  	{
  434  		int idx = 0;
  435  		for (auto system : SystemData::sSystemVector)
  436  		{
  437  			cleanupGamelist(system);
  438  			idx++;
  439  		}
  440  		}, _("NO"), nullptr));
  441  	});
  442  
  443  	s->addGroup(_("EMULATOR MANAGEMENT"));
  444  	s->addEntry(_("RESET RETROARCH CONFIG TO DEFAULT"), true, [window] {
  445  	window->pushGui(new GuiMsgBox(window, _("WARNING: RETROARCH CONFIG WILL RESET TO DEFAULT\n\nPER-CORE CONFIGURATIONS WILL NOT BE AFFECTED AND NO BACKUP WILL BE CREATED!\n\nRESET RETROARCH CONFIG TO DEFAULT?"), _("YES"),
  446  		[window] {
  447  		runMaintenanceCommand(window, "/usr/bin/factoryreset retroarch", _("RESETTING RETROARCH..."),
  448  			_("RETROARCH CONFIG RESET TO DEFAULT."),
  449  			_("THE RETROARCH CONFIG COULDN'T BE RESET."));
  450  		}, _("NO"), nullptr));
  451  	});
  452  
  453  	s->addEntry(_("RESET OVERLAYS (CORES, CHEATS, JOYPADS, ETC)"), true, [window] {
```

```text
  682  void GuiMenu::exitKidMode()
  683  {
  684  	if (Settings::getInstance()->getString("UIMode") == "Basic")
```

```text
 4078  // A row that is there and not for pressing: dimmed, its one line saying
 4079  // why (D-CLOUD-162's SETTINGS row with no backup from this device). Not
 4080  // selectable, so the cursor passes over it; dimmed by the entry on every
 4081  // frame, as the gated rows are (fork #182). A row that is not selectable
 4082  // is laid out from the list's edge with no inset -- the section headers
 4083  // pad their own text -- so this one carries the selectable rows' inset on
 4084  // both sides itself; without it the row sat flush with the panel's edge,
 4085  // out of line with the rows above it (guest d, run 95; vm-qa run 97).
 4086  static void cloudAddDimmedRow(GuiSettings* s, Window* window, const std::string& label, const std::string& description)
 4087  {
 4088  	ComponentListRow row;
 4089  	row.selectable = false;
 4090  	auto left = std::make_shared<GuiComponent>(window);
 4091  	left->setSize(ComponentList::rowInset(), 0);
 4092  	row.addElement(left, false);
 4093  	auto entry = std::make_shared<MultiLineMenuEntry>(window, Utils::String::toUpper(label), description, true);
 4094  	entry->setDimmed(true);
 4095  	row.addElement(entry, true);
 4096  	auto right = std::make_shared<GuiComponent>(window);
 4097  	right->setSize(ComponentList::rowInset(), 0);
 4098  	row.addElement(right, false);
 4099  	s->addRow(row);
 4100  }
 4101  static std::string cloudShellQuote(const std::string& value);
 4102  
 4103  // The picker's selection, handed to the content script as it closes. The
 4104  // names are folder names in the player's cloud (--scan's first field) --
 4105  // the owner's own, or another of their devices', and still data: inside
 4106  // double quotes $(, backticks and " stayed live for the shell
 4107  // executeScriptLegacy runs, so a folder named a$(reboot)b ran (audit #307
 4108  // PL-030). One single-quoted argument, as the script has always taken
 4109  // them (it splits on the spaces itself); what it accepts as a name is the
 4110  // script's to refuse.
 4111  static std::string cloudSetSystemsCommand(const std::vector<std::string>& names)
 4112  {
 4113  	std::string picked;
 4114  	for (auto& name : names)
 4115  		picked += (picked.empty() ? "" : " ") + name;
 4116  	return "/usr/bin/cloud_content_restore --set-systems " + cloudShellQuote(picked);
 4117  }
 4118  
 4119  // Where --set-systems keeps the selection (cloud_content_restore's
 4120  // SELECTION), which --selected reads when the run starts. A macro so a test
 4121  // can point it elsewhere (tests/cloud-content-selection.py).
 4122  #ifndef CLOUD_CONTENT_SELECTION
 4123  #define CLOUD_CONTENT_SELECTION "/storage/.cache/cloud_sync/content-systems"
 4124  #endif
 4125  
 4126  // The selection file's names, one a line. An ifstream, not readAllText:
 4127  // that reads a file under three bytes as empty (es-code-traps.md), and a
 4128  // one-letter name makes a two-byte file.
 4129  // The names in the selection file, and whether the file was read whole:
 4130  // a path that is not a regular file (a directory opens, then getline
 4131  // fails) or a read that ends in an error is not an empty selection (the
 4132  // audit of the fixes, gpt G3-E-03).
 4133  static bool cloudSelectionRead(const std::string& path, std::vector<std::string>& names)
 4134  {
 4135  	names.clear();
 4136  	if (!Utils::FileSystem::isRegularFile(path))
 4137  		return false;
 4138  	std::ifstream in(path);
 4139  	if (!in.is_open())
 4140  		return false;
 4141  	std::string line;
 4142  	while (std::getline(in, line))
 4143  	{
 4144  		if (!line.empty() && line.back() == '\r')
 4145  			line.pop_back();
 4146  		if (!line.empty())
 4147  			names.push_back(line);
 4148  	}
 4149  	return in.eof() && !in.bad();
 4150  }
 4151  
 4152  // What the scan page wrote (cloud_scan, fork #350): the options page and the
 4153  // systems page are built from these files, never from a loader of their own.
 4154  // Read with the selection reader above -- line by line, so a file under
 4155  // three bytes is not read as empty (es-code-traps.md) -- and uncached: the
 4156  // scan writes them while this process runs.
 4157  static const char* CLOUD_SCAN_DIR = "/storage/.cache/cloud_sync/scan";
 4158  static bool cloudScanLines(const std::string& name, std::vector<std::string>& lines)
 4159  {
 4160  	return cloudSelectionRead(std::string(CLOUD_SCAN_DIR) + "/" + name, lines);
 4161  }
 4162  static std::map<std::string, std::string> cloudScanFacts(const std::string& name)
 4163  {
 4164  	std::vector<std::string> lines;
 4165  	cloudScanLines(name, lines);
 4166  	std::string text;
 4167  	for (auto& l : lines)
 4168  		text += l + "\n";
 4169  	return CloudText::parseKeyValues(text);
 4170  }
 4171  static std::string cloudScanFact(const std::map<std::string, std::string>& facts, const std::string& key)
 4172  {
 4173  	auto it = facts.find(key);
 4174  	return it == facts.end() ? "" : it->second;
 4175  }
 4176  static std::string sCloudScanRun, sCloudContentScanRun, sCloudContentScanMode;
 4177  static std::string cloudNewCheckId()
 4178  {
 4179  	static unsigned long sequence = 0;
 4180  	return "es-" + std::to_string(std::chrono::steady_clock::now().time_since_epoch().count())
 4181  		+ "-" + std::to_string(++sequence);
 4182  }
 4183  static bool cloudScanStamped(const std::string& name)
 4184  {
 4185  	const auto& run = name == "content-done" ? sCloudContentScanRun : sCloudScanRun;
 4186  	if (run.empty()) return false;
 4187  	const std::string mode = name == "content-done" ? sCloudContentScanMode : "";
 4188  	return ApiSystem::executeScriptLegacy("timeout 5 /usr/bin/cloud_scan --stamp-valid "
 4189  		+ cloudShellQuote(name) + " " + cloudShellQuote(run) + mode,
 4190  		[](const std::string&) {}).second == 0;
 4191  }
 4192  static void cloudCheckChanged(Window* window)
 4193  {
 4194  	window->pushGui(new GuiMsgBox(window,
 4195  		_("THE CLOUD CHECK IS NO LONGER CURRENT. CHECK YOUR FOLDERS AGAIN."), _("OK")));
 4196  }
 4197  // The picker's selection, saved and then read back before anything moves on
 4198  // it (audit of the fix round PL-019). --set-systems exits 0 over a rename
 4199  // that failed -- a full card, a folder gone read-only -- and the file then
 4200  // still holds the last run's picks, which --selected carries: systems the
 4201  // player did not tick, in either direction. So the answer is the file, not
 4202  // the exit status alone. True when the file names exactly what was ticked;
 4203  // nothing ticked is a file with nothing in it, never no file (the script
 4204  // writes one either way).
 4205  static bool cloudSaveSelection(const std::vector<std::string>& names)
 4206  {
 4207  	const int rc = ApiSystem::executeScriptLegacy(cloudSetSystemsCommand(names), nullptr).second;
 4208  	if (rc != 0)
 4209  	{
 4210  		LOG(LogError) << "cloud content: --set-systems exited " << rc << "; the selection was not saved";
 4211  		return false;
 4212  	}
 4213  	std::vector<std::string> saved;
 4214  	if (!cloudSelectionRead(CLOUD_CONTENT_SELECTION, saved) || std::set<std::string>(saved.begin(), saved.end()) != std::set<std::string>(names.begin(), names.end()))
 4215  	{
 4216  		LOG(LogError) << "cloud content: --set-systems exited 0, but " << CLOUD_CONTENT_SELECTION << " does not name what was ticked";
 4217  		return false;
 4218  	}
 4219  	return true;
 4220  }
 4221  
 4222  // What the picker says when the selection could not be saved: the why, and
 4223  // that nothing started. The page stays, and its verb is the way to try again.
 4224  static std::string cloudSelectionNotSaved(bool backup)
 4225  {
 4226  	return backup ? _("COULDN'T SAVE WHAT YOU TICKED, SO NOTHING WAS BACKED UP.")
 4227  	              : _("COULDN'T SAVE WHAT YOU TICKED, SO NOTHING WAS RESTORED.");
 4228  }
 4229  
 4230  // Horizontal padding matching what ComponentList applies to selectable
 4231  // rows, so informational text lines up with the actionable rows.
 4232  #define CLOUD_SETUP_ROW_PADDING Vector4f(10, 0, 10, 0)
 4233  static void cloudSetupAddInfoRow(GuiSettings* s, Window* window, const std::string& text, bool accent);
 4234  
 4235  // Which systems this device syncs.
 4236  //
```

```text
 4243  //
 4244  // The scan reads sizes because without them "should I take PSX?" cannot be
 4245  // answered. It is one recursive listing rather than a size call per system,
 4246  // but it is still a network round trip, so it runs behind GuiLoading.
 4247  // The content scripts' mode for a pair of ticks (D-CLOUD-050): ROMs and
 4248  // BIOS alone is the default; game content rides along with --with-media, or
 4249  // moves on its own with --media-only. The game list is game content
 4250  // (D-CLOUD-049), so a ROMs-only transfer leaves it where it is.
 4251  static std::string cloudContentMode(bool content, bool media)
 4252  {
 4253  	if (content && media)
 4254  		return " --with-media";
 4255  	if (media)
 4256  		return " --media-only";
 4257  	return "";
 4258  }
 4259  
 4260  // One page per direction (D-CLOUD-048). CONTENT TO BACK UP lists what this
 4261  // device holds; CONTENT TO RESTORE lists what the cloud holds. Both sides are
 4262  // listed by the transfer's own rule and compared by file name and size,
 4263  // because totals cannot say whether one side has what the other has -- a
 4264  // restored-then-scraped device read "different size" on every system for
 4265  // that reason. Each row's line leads with what this run would move, since
 4266  // that is the question a player on this page is asking (maintainer,
 4267  // 2026-09-08: "what they'll want to know is the delta, or what's being sent
 4268  // up, not just what's in their cloud"; 2026-09-09: "instead of saying
 4269  // 'already in your cloud,' what we're trying to address is 'not yet in your
 4270  // cloud'" -- D-UI-027).
 4271  // A centred, unselectable line of standard text: what a page says about
 4272  // itself before its rows begin.
 4273  static void cloudAddCentredLine(GuiSettings* s, Window* window, const std::string& text)
 4274  {
 4275  	auto theme = ThemeData::getMenuTheme();
 4276  	ComponentListRow row;
 4277  	row.selectable = false;
 4278  	auto tc = std::make_shared<TextComponent>(window, text, theme->Text.font, theme->Text.color, ALIGN_CENTER);
 4279  	tc->setPadding(CLOUD_SETUP_ROW_PADDING);
 4280  	row.addElement(tc, true);
 4281  	s->addRow(row);
 4282  }
 4283  
 4284  // What --scan said and how it ended. The exit code is the difference between
 4285  // an empty cloud and a cloud that could not be read (below).
 4286  struct CloudScanResult
 4287  {
 4288  	std::vector<std::string> scan;
 4289  	int rc = -1;
 4290  	std::vector<std::string> selected;
 4291  };
 4292  
 4293  static void cloudContentSystemPicker(Window* window, const std::function<void()>& onDone, const std::string& proceedLabel, bool backup, bool content, bool media, const std::string& perSystem, const std::string& wholeDevice)
 4294  {
 4295  	// The listing is the content scan's, run on a page of its own before
 4296  	// this is built (cloud_scan --content, #350): the page is built from
 4297  	// its files. A loader over the options page was the pop-up over a
 4298  	// pop-up the maintainer met (2026-09-30); it remains only for scripts
 4299  	// older than cloud_scan.
 4300  	auto build = [window, onDone, proceedLabel, backup, content, perSystem, wholeDevice](CloudScanResult result)
 4301  		{
 4302  			// A scan that could not read the cloud says so, and says what to
 4303  			// do; only a scan that read it and found nothing shows the
 4304  			// nothing-found text below (D-CLOUD-077: a scan that fails must
 4305  			// say so, not show an empty cloud).
 4306  			if (result.rc == CloudExit::NoNetwork)
 4307  			{
 4308  				window->pushGui(new GuiMsgBox(window, _("COULDN'T REACH YOUR CLOUD.\n\nTRY AGAIN WHEN YOU'RE ONLINE.")));
 4309  				return;
 4310  			}
 4311  			if (result.rc != 0)
 4312  			{
 4313  				window->pushGui(new GuiMsgBox(window, _("COULDN'T READ YOUR CLOUD'S CONTENT.\n\nTRY AGAIN.")));
 4314  				return;
 4315  			}
 4316  
 4317  			std::set<std::string> chosen;
 4318  			for (auto& line : result.selected)
 4319  			{
 4320  				auto name = Utils::String::trim(line);
 4321  				if (!name.empty())
 4322  					chosen.insert(name);
 4323  			}
 4324  			// name|cloud_bytes|supported|device_bytes|files_in_cloud_not_here|files_here_not_in_cloud|bytes_in_cloud_not_here|bytes_here_not_in_cloud
 4325  			// Fields 7 and 8 -- the bytes a copy in each direction would send --
 4326  			// arrived with the scripts of 2026-09-08. `sized` records whether
 4327  			// this scan carried them, so a page running ahead of its scripts
 4328  			// falls back to the count-only verdict rather than reading two
 4329  			// missing fields as "nothing to move".
 4330  			struct Found { std::string name; unsigned long cloudBytes; bool supported; unsigned long localBytes; int cloudNotHere; int hereNotCloud; unsigned long cloudNotHereBytes; unsigned long hereNotCloudBytes; bool sized; };
 4331  			std::vector<Found> found;
 4332  			Found bios{ "bios", 0, true, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, false };
 4333  			bool biosListed = false;
 4334  			for (auto& line : result.scan)
 4335  			{
 4336  				auto p = Utils::String::split(Utils::String::trim(line), '|', true);
 4337  				if (p.size() < 3)
 4338  					continue;
 4339  				Found f{ p[0], (unsigned long) atol(p[1].c_str()), p[2] == "1", 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, false };
 4340  				if (p.size() >= 6)
 4341  				{
 4342  					f.localBytes   = (unsigned long) atol(p[3].c_str());
 4343  					f.cloudNotHere = atoi(p[4].c_str());
 4344  					f.hereNotCloud = atoi(p[5].c_str());
 4345  				}
 4346  				if (p.size() >= 8)
 4347  				{
 4348  					f.cloudNotHereBytes = (unsigned long) atol(p[6].c_str());
 4349  					f.hereNotCloudBytes = (unsigned long) atol(p[7].c_str());
 4350  					f.sized = true;
 4351  				}
 4352  				if (f.name == "bios")
 4353  				{
 4354  					// Not a system; comes with the tier (D-CLOUD-043). Kept
 4355  					// aside for the case below.
 4356  					bios = f;
 4357  					biosListed = true;
 4358  					continue;
 4359  				}
 4360  				// Each direction lists only what it can act on: backup sends what
 4361  				// is here, restore brings what is there. A file counts when it
 4362  				// would move, whatever its size: a system whose only file is
 4363  				// empty read as nothing to move and was left out (#308 8a gpt
 4364  				// F-ES-10).
 4365  				if (backup ? (f.localBytes == 0 && f.hereNotCloud == 0) : (f.cloudBytes == 0 && f.cloudNotHere == 0))
 4366  					continue;
 4367  				found.push_back(f);
 4368  			}
 4369  			// One line under the label (D-UI-023), and it answers the one
 4370  			// question this page asks: is there anything not yet on the
 4371  			// far side, and how much? "253.9 MB NOT YET IN YOUR CLOUD . 14
 4372  			// FILES" when there is, NOTHING NEW TO BACK UP when there is
 4373  			// not; the restore page reads NOT YET ON THIS DEVICE / NOTHING
 4374  			// NEW TO RESTORE. No total anywhere on the row (D-UI-027): the
 4375  			// zero case used to read ALREADY IN YOUR CLOUD (253.9 MB), and
 4376  			// a size beside a system reads as an amount about to move --
 4377  			// "does that mean I'm backing up 253.9 MB?" (maintainer,
 4378  			// 2026-09-09). The count stays because a delta of many tiny
 4379  			// files is a different job from one big file. Sizes are the
 4380  			// transfer page's (GuiCloudTransfer::sizeLabel): a whole KB
 4381  			// below a megabyte, rounded up so a one-byte difference never
 4382  			// reads "0 KB", and no decimals that could only ever be zero
 4383  			// (#85). A difference of only empty files is carried by the
 4384  			// count instead. The BIOS row, when it is the only thing to
 4385  			// move (below), reads the same way.
 4386  			auto noteFor = [backup](const Found& f)
 4387  			{
 4388  				const unsigned long total = backup ? f.localBytes : f.cloudBytes;
 4389  				const unsigned long other = backup ? f.cloudBytes : f.localBytes;
 4390  				// The bytes a copy in this direction would send. A six-field
 4391  				// scan, from scripts older than this page, has no such figure:
 4392  				// its row is carried by the count alone rather than by a total
 4393  				// that says nothing about what moves.
 4394  				unsigned long delta = !f.sized ? 0 : backup ? f.hereNotCloudBytes : f.cloudNotHereBytes;
 4395  				const int count = backup ? f.hereNotCloud : f.cloudNotHere;
 4396  				// Nothing on the far side means all of it moves. That is true
 4397  				// by construction, needs no byte fields, and is the only true
 4398  				// reading of a scan line that was never compared (the pre-tier
 4399  				// layout, which the script lists for size alone).
 4400  				if (other == 0)
 4401  					delta = total;
 4402  				std::string note;
 4403  				if (delta > 0 || count > 0)
 4404  				{
 4405  					// The size leads when there is one. A file the far side
 4406  					// lacks can be empty -- pico-8 ships a 0-byte Splore.png --
 4407  					// and a copy sends it all the same, so when the bytes are
 4408  					// 0 the count carries the line ("1 FILE NOT YET IN YOUR
 4409  					// CLOUD") rather than the row reading NOTHING NEW over a
 4410  					// file that would move, or "0.00 KB" beside it. Beside THIS
 4411  					// DEVICE CANNOT RUN IT the count is noise, and size, count
 4412  					// and suffix together run a 640px row to a third line
 4413  					// (D-UI-023), so the count is dropped there.
 4414  					const std::string where = backup ? _("NOT YET IN YOUR CLOUD") : _("NOT YET ON THIS DEVICE");
 4415  					const std::string files = std::to_string(count) + " " + (count == 1 ? _("FILE") : _("FILES"));
 4416  					if (delta == 0)
 4417  						note = files + " " + where;
 4418  					else if (count > 0 && f.supported)
 4419  						note = GuiCloudTransfer::sizeLabel(delta) + " " + where + " · " + files;
 4420  					else
 4421  						note = GuiCloudTransfer::sizeLabel(delta) + " " + where;
 4422  				}
 4423  				else
 4424  					note = backup ? _("NOTHING NEW TO BACK UP") : _("NOTHING NEW TO RESTORE");
 4425  				if (!f.supported)
 4426  					note += " · " + _("THIS DEVICE CANNOT RUN IT");
 4427  				return note;
 4428  			};
 4429  			// BIOS files and no system to pick: the tier's other half still
 4430  			// moves (#308 8a gpt F-ES-10). It used to end in NO SYSTEM ...
 4431  			// HOLDS WHAT YOU TICKED with the BIOS files sitting there. The
 4432  			// selection names bios alone: cloud_content_restore --selected
 4433  			// refuses an empty selection before it adds bios, and both
 4434  			// scripts take bios in the selection as the tier. Not for game

 4449  				auto s = new GuiSettings(window, backup ? _("CONTENT TO BACK UP") : _("CONTENT TO RESTORE"));

 4460  				if (onDone)
 4461  					s->getMenu().addButton(proceedLabel.empty() ? _("CONTINUE") : proceedLabel, _("continue"),
 4462  						[window, s, proceed, backup]
 4463  						{
 4464  							if (!cloudSaveSelection({ "bios" }))

 4467  								return;

 4476  			if (found.empty())
 4477  			{
```

```text
 4626  	time_t when = 0;
 4627  	int code = 0;
 4628  	std::string token;
 4629  	std::string why;      // upper case, no trailing period; empty when it completed
 4630  	std::string outcome;  // the row's word(s): COMPLETED, COULDN'T FINISH, SKIPPED, ...
 4631  	bool finished = false;
 4632  };
 4633  
 4634  static CloudLastRun cloudReadLastRun(const std::string& name)
 4635  {
 4636  	CloudLastRun r;
 4637  	const std::string path = "/storage/.cache/cloud_sync/last-" + name;
 4638  	// Uncached: the stamps are written by the scripts and by
 4639  	// ThreadedCloudSync while this process runs, and Utils::FileSystem::exists
 4640  	// remembers a miss (UseFileCache) until a game launch or a restart clears
 4641  	// it. A page opened once before a run read NOT DONE ON THIS DEVICE YET
 4642  	// after it, stamp on disk or not (guest d, 2026-09-10). The same goes for
 4643  	// every file another process writes: rclone.conf below, once the wizard
 4644  	// has made it.
 4645  	if (!Utils::FileSystem::exists(path, false))
 4646  		return r;
 4647  	// The line itself -- its fields, its two shapes of third field, and
 4648  	// which of the four outcomes it names -- is parsed in CloudText, where
 4649  	// a test can reach it without a device or a locale (es-app/tests/unit).
 4650  	// What stays here is the read, the fallback why for a stamp that gave
 4651  	// none, and the outcome said in the player's language -- the why too:
 4652  	// the stamp keeps the script's English sentence for its readers, and
 4653  	// CloudText::localizedWhy turns it into the interface's language, as the
 4654  	// card does (#308 F-CS-31; one it does not list comes back as it was).
 4655  	const CloudText::LastRun p = CloudText::parseLastRun(Utils::FileSystem::readAllText(path));
 4656  	if (!p.ran)
 4657  		return r;
 4658  
 4659  	r.ran = true;
 4660  	r.when = p.when;
 4661  	r.code = p.code;
 4662  	r.token = p.token;
 4663  	r.finished = p.finished;
 4664  
 4665  	switch (p.outcome)
 4666  	{
 4667  	case CloudText::Outcome::Completed:
 4668  		r.outcome = _("COMPLETED");
 4669  		break;
 4670  	case CloudText::Outcome::Gaps:
 4671  		// A run whose parts disagreed. The stamp keeps its own token so a log
 4672  		// can tell it from a total failure; the row says what every other
 4673  		// failure says (D-UI-030).
 4674  		r.outcome = _("COULDN'T FINISH");
 4675  		r.why = p.why.empty() ? ThreadedCloudSync::whyForToken(p.token) : CloudText::localizedWhy(p.why);
 4676  		break;
 4677  	case CloudText::Outcome::SkippedLockHeld:
 4678  		r.outcome = _("SKIPPED, ANOTHER SYNC WAS RUNNING");
 4679  		break;
 4680  	case CloudText::Outcome::SkippedNoNetwork:
 4681  		r.outcome = _("SKIPPED, NO NETWORK");
 4682  		break;
 4683  	case CloudText::Outcome::SkippedGameStarted:
 4684  		r.outcome = _("SKIPPED, A GAME WAS STARTED");
 4685  		break;
 4686  	case CloudText::Outcome::SkippedCancelled:
 4687  		r.outcome = _("SKIPPED, YOU CANCELLED IT");
 4688  		break;
 4689  	case CloudText::Outcome::SkippedNoFolder:
 4690  		// An automatic sync with no saves folder in the cloud yet (D-CLOUD-166):
 4691  		// the card's SKIPPED word, with the row's comma.
 4692  		r.outcome = _("SKIPPED, YOUR CLOUD FOLDER ISN'T SET UP YET");
 4693  		break;
 4694  	case CloudText::Outcome::Failed:
 4695  	{
 4696  		// The token's phrase for one of ours, the code's for anything else.
 4697  		std::string why = CloudText::localizedWhy(p.why);
 4698  		if (why.empty() && p.knownToken)
 4699  			why = ThreadedCloudSync::whyForToken(p.token);
 4700  		if (why.empty())
 4701  			why = ThreadedCloudSync::whyForCode(p.code);
 4702  		r.outcome = _("COULDN'T FINISH");
 4703  		r.why = why;
 4704  		break;
 4705  	}
 4706  	}
 4707  	return r;
 4708  }
 4709  
 4710  // The line under a row: the date and how it ended, one line (D-UI-023).
 4711  //
 4712  // This used to be its own menu row -- LAST CONTENT UPLOAD sitting in a list
 4713  // next to the actions, which is where you put a fact you have nowhere better
 4714  // for. It belongs on the row it describes: the answer to "should I back this
 4715  // up?" is "you last did, and it worked", read in the moment of deciding.
 4716  // Was the run this stamp records one the player started, or one of the two
```

```text
 4719  // last-backup/last-restore, so a matching time says which it was. Inferred
 4720  // rather than recorded because the scripts do not know who called them --
 4721  // and it only ever changes the words, never the outcome.
 4722  //
 4723  // It exists because the page contradicted itself: BACK UP SAVES TO THE CLOUD
 4724  // reported the sync that runs when you exit a game, under a label naming
 4725  // something the player never pressed, while SYNC SAVES WITH THE CLOUD said
 4726  // NOT DONE ON THIS DEVICE YET (maintainer, 2026-09-10, fork #112).
 4727  static std::string cloudRunOrigin(time_t when)
 4728  {
 4729  	if (when <= 0)
 4730  		return "";
 4731  	// In preference order, and each stamp read only if it is still needed:
 4732  	// the exit stamp answers most rows, and the window that makes two
 4733  	// stamps the same run is CloudText's (es-app/tests/unit checks its
 4734  	// edges).
 4735  	const CloudLastRun exitRun = cloudReadLastRun("sync-exit");
 4736  	if (CloudText::runOrigin(when, exitRun.ran ? exitRun.when : 0, 0) == CloudText::RunOrigin::AfterLastGame)
 4737  		return _("AFTER YOUR LAST GAME");
 4738  
 4739  	const CloudLastRun startupRun = cloudReadLastRun("sync-startup");
 4740  	if (CloudText::runOrigin(when, 0, startupRun.ran ? startupRun.when : 0) == CloudText::RunOrigin::AtStartup)
 4741  		return _("AT STARTUP");
 4742  
 4743  	return "";
 4744  }
 4745  
 4746  // "LAST <date>" in the player's own date format and clock, not ours.
 4747  //
 4748  // timeToString already resolves through localtime(), so the timezone comes
 4749  // from /etc/localtime and was never wrong; the hardcoded "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M"
 4750  // was. Somebody who set SHOW CLOCK IN 12-HOUR FORMAT gets 24-hour times
 4751  // here and nowhere else in the app, which reads as a bug in the row rather
 4752  // than a setting that did not reach it.
 4753  //
 4754  // The date half comes from the system locale (getSystemDateFormat caches,
 4755  // which is fine -- a locale does not change while the menu is open). The
 4756  // time half is decided live, because ClockMode12 is a switch the player can
 4757  // flip and come straight back to this page. One helper, so the row under a
 4758  // transfer whose outcome page has not yet been dismissed (fork #187,
 4759  // D-UI-070) dates its outcome the way every other LAST row does.
 4760  static std::string cloudLastLabel(time_t when)
 4761  {
 4762  	const std::string fmt = Utils::Time::getSystemDateFormat()
 4763  		+ (Settings::ClockMode12() ? " %I:%M %p" : " %H:%M");
 4764  	return _("LAST") + std::string(" ") + Utils::Time::timeToString(when, fmt);
 4765  }
 4766  
 4767  // The run a row reports. SYNC SAVES WITH THE CLOUD moves saves both ways,
 4768  // and so do the two automatic syncs; a row that counted only the manual one
 4769  // said NOT DONE ON THIS DEVICE YET on a device that had been syncing all
 4770  // along, so it reports the newest sync by any route (#112). The row's line
 4771  // and its confirmation's LAST TIME both read this one, so they speak of the
 4772  // same run: the confirmation read the manual stamp alone, and could explain
 4773  // a failure the row no longer showed (#308 8a gpt F-ES-16).
 4774  static CloudLastRun cloudLatestRun(const std::string& name)
 4775  {
 4776  	CloudLastRun r = cloudReadLastRun(name);
 4777  	if (name == "sync-manual")
 4778  		for (auto* other : { "sync-exit", "sync-startup" })
 4779  		{
 4780  			const CloudLastRun a = cloudReadLastRun(other);
 4781  			if (a.ran && (!r.ran || a.when > r.when))
 4782  				r = a;
 4783  		}
 4784  	return r;
 4785  }
 4786  
 4787  static std::string cloudLastRunDetail(const std::string& name)
 4788  {
 4789  	const CloudLastRun r = cloudLatestRun(name);
 4790  	if (!r.ran)
 4791  		return _("NOT DONE ON THIS DEVICE YET");
 4792  	const time_t when = r.when;
 4793  
 4794  	// An automatic run says which one it was instead of its date: the date is
 4795  	// what the player would have had to reason from to work that out, and the
 4796  	// row has two lines to say it in (D-UI-023).
 4797  	const std::string origin = cloudRunOrigin(when);
 4798  	if (!origin.empty())
 4799  		return origin + "  -  " + r.outcome;
 4800  
 4801  	return cloudLastLabel(when) + "  -  " + r.outcome;
 4802  }
 4803  
 4804  // The second paragraph of the confirmation dialog: why the last run could
 4805  // not finish, so the decision to try again is made knowing what stopped the
 4806  // last one. Empty when it finished, was skipped, or gave no reason -- the
 4807  // dialog then reads as it always has.
 4808  static std::string cloudLastRunWhy(const std::string& name)
 4809  {
 4810  	const CloudLastRun r = cloudLatestRun(name);
 4811  	if (!r.ran || r.why.empty())
 4812  		return "";
 4813  	return std::string("\n\n") + _("LAST TIME IT COULDN'T FINISH:") + " " + r.why + ".";
 4814  }
```
